7
Conclusion

In this work, we proposed a general framework for addressing continuous CRL problems via
primal-dual policy-based
algorithms, employing an alternating ascent–descent scheme. Our
exploration-agnostic
algorithm,
C-PG
, provides
dimension-free, global, last-iterate
convergence guarantees under the standard weak gradient domination assumption.
Furthermore, we reinterpreted both
action-based
and
parameter-based
exploration paradigms as white-noise perturbations applied to parametric deterministic policies, either at the action level or at the parameter one. Under this noise model, we established all the conditions required to ensure last-iterate convergence of
C-PG
, and we proved that
C-PG
converges to an optimal deterministic policy when trained via a stochastic (hyper)policy and the stochasticity is subsequently switched off at the end of the learning phase.
We validated our theoretical findings by comparing our methods against state-of-the-art baselines and demonstrating their effectiveness, particularly in the deployment of deterministic policies.
Future research should aim to improve
sample complexity
of
C-PG
, with the goal of matching the lower bounds established by
Vaswani et al. 2022
. Another promising direction is the development of
single time-scale algorithms
that retain the same convergence guarantees.
Finally, our analysis assumes a fixed level of stochasticity
\sigma
, which must be set on the order of
\mathcal{O}(\epsilon)
to guarantee convergence to an optimal deterministic policy under the adopted noise model. However, this assumption may be impractical in real-world scenarios, where stochasticity is often either learned or gradually annealed during training. This gap between theory and practice has recently been addressed by
Montenegro et al. 2025
in the unconstrained setting; extending this line of work to the constrained case remains an important direction for future research.

Appendix A
Omitted Proofs

Lemma A.1

(Regularization Bias on Saddle Points - 1)
.

Under Assumption
3.1
, for every
\omega\geq 0
, let
(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})
be a saddle point of
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}
, it holds that:

\displaystyle 0\leq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})-\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})\leq\frac{\omega}{2}\left(\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega}\|_{2}^{2}\right).

Proof.

From the fact that
(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})
is a saddle point of
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}
, we have for every
{\bm{\upsilon}}\in\mathcal{V}
and
{\bm{\lambda}}{\in\Lambda}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\omega}({\bm{\upsilon}},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})\geq\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})\geq\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},{\bm{\lambda}})

(9)

\displaystyle\iff\mathcal{L}_{0}({\bm{\upsilon}},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})-\frac{\omega}{2}\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})-\frac{\omega}{2}\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},{\bm{\lambda}})-\frac{\omega}{2}\left\|{\bm{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}^{2}

(10)

\displaystyle\iff\mathcal{L}_{0}({\bm{\upsilon}},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},{\bm{\lambda}})+\frac{\omega}{2}\left(\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|{\bm{\lambda}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right).

(11)

From the fact that
(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})
is a saddle point of
\mathcal{L}_{0}
, we have for every
{\bm{\upsilon}}\in\mathcal{V}
and
{\bm{\lambda}}{\in\Lambda}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}({\bm{\upsilon}},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})\geq

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},{\bm{\lambda}}).

(12)

By setting
(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\leftarrow(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})
in Equation (
12
) and
(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\leftarrow(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})

in Equation (
11
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})

(13)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})+\frac{\omega}{2}\left(\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right)

(14)

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})+\frac{\omega}{2}\left(\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right),

(15)

thus:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})\geq\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})+\frac{\omega}{2}\left(\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega}\right\|_{2}^{2}-\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right).

(16)

∎

Lemma A.2

(Regularization Bias on Saddle Points - 2)
.

Under Assumption
3.1
, for every
\omega\geq 0
, it holds that:

\displaystyle 0\leq\min_{\bm{\upsilon}\in\mathcal{V}}\max_{\bm{\lambda}{\in\Lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})-\min_{\bm{\upsilon}\in\mathcal{V}}\max_{\bm{\lambda}{\in\Lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\leq\frac{\omega}{2}\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}.

Proof.

The first inequality follows from the observation that
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\geq\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})
for every
\omega\geq 0
.
For the second inequality, let us denote as
(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})
the saddle point for
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}

and let
\Lambda^{*}=\left\{\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega}\right\}
.
We have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})

\displaystyle=\min_{\bm{\upsilon}\in\mathcal{V}}\max_{\bm{\lambda}\in\Lambda^{*}}\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})-\min_{\bm{\upsilon}\in\mathcal{V}}\max_{\bm{\lambda}\in\Lambda^{*}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\

(17)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\bm{\upsilon}\in\mathcal{V}}\left|\max_{\bm{\lambda}\in\Lambda^{*}}\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})-\max_{\bm{\lambda}\in\Lambda^{*}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\right|

(18)

\displaystyle=\max_{\bm{\upsilon}\in\mathcal{V},\bm{\lambda}\in\Lambda^{*}}\left|\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\right|

(19)

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega}{2}\max\left\{\left\|\bm{\lambda}_{0}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2};\;\left\|\bm{\lambda}_{\omega}^{*}\right\|_{2}^{2}\right\}

(20)

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega}{2}\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\right\|_{2}^{2},

(21)

where we used Lemma
A.1
to conclude that
\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega}\right\|_{2}^{2}
.
∎

Lemma A.3

(Objective bound and Constraint violation)
.

Under Assumption
3.1
, for every
\omega\geq 0
, letting
(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})
be a saddle point of
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}
, it holds that:

\displaystyle 0\leq J_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0})-J_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})\leq\omega\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\|_{2}^{2},

(22)

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b})^{+}\|_{2}\leq\omega\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\|_{2}.

(23)

Proof.

Since
(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})
is a saddle point of
\mathcal{L}_{0}
, it holds that
\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0}
is feasible and, consequently,
\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0})=J_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0})
. Moreover, let
\omega>0
: since
(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})
is a saddle point of
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}
it holds that
\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega}=\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})=\Pi_{\Lambda}\left(\frac{1}{\omega}(\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b})\right)=\frac{1}{\omega}(\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b})^{+}
, since
\frac{1}{\omega}\|(\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b})^{+}\|_{2}\leq\omega^{-1}\sqrt{U}J_{\max}
. Thus, we have:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})=J_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})+\langle\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega},\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b}\rangle=J_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})+\frac{1}{\omega}\|(\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b})^{+}\|_{2}^{2}.

(24)

From Lemma
A.1
, we have:

\displaystyle 0\leq J_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0})-J_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\frac{1}{\omega}\|(\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b})^{+}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{\omega}{2}\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{2\omega}\|(\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b})^{+}\|_{2}^{2}.

(25)

By summing
\frac{1}{\omega}\|(\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b})^{+}\|_{2}^{2}
to all members, we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega}\|(\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b})^{+}\|_{2}^{2}\leq J_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0})-J_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})\leq\frac{\omega}{2}\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{2\omega}\|(\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b})^{+}\|_{2}^{2}.

(26)

Now taking the first and last member, we conclude:

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b})^{+}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\omega^{2}\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}.

(27)

Since
\frac{1}{\omega}\|(\mathbf{J}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})-\mathbf{b})^{+}\|_{2}^{2}\geq 0
and plugging the latter inequality into the third member of (
26
) we obtain:

\displaystyle 0\leq J_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{0})-J_{0}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega})\leq\omega\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{0}\|_{2}^{2}.

(28)

∎

Lemma A.4

(Weak
\psi
-Gradient Domination on
H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})
)
.

Under Assumption
3.2
, if
\omega>0
, for every
\bm{\upsilon}\in\mathcal{V}
and
\bm{\lambda}{\in\Lambda}
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})\right\|_{2}^{\psi}\geq\alpha_{1}\left(H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-\min_{\bm{\upsilon}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}^{\prime})\right)-\beta_{1}.

(29)

Proof.

If
\omega>0
, the dual variable exist finite since the maximization problem over
\bm{\lambda}
is concave:

\displaystyle\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon})=\argmax_{\bm{\lambda}{\in\Lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda}).

Thus, we have from Lemma
A.7
that
\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})=\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\rvert_{\bm{\lambda}=\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon})}
and by Assumption
3.2
we have the following:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\rvert_{\bm{\lambda}=\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon})}\right\|_{2}

(30)

\displaystyle\geq\alpha_{1}\left(\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon}))-\min_{{\bm{\upsilon}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}({\bm{\upsilon}^{\prime}},\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon}))\right)-\beta_{1}

(31)

\displaystyle\geq\alpha_{1}\left(H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-\min_{{\bm{\upsilon}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}}}\max_{\bm{\lambda}{\in\Lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}({\bm{\upsilon}^{\prime}},\bm{\lambda})\right)-\beta_{1}

(32)

\displaystyle=\alpha_{1}\left(H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-H^{*}_{\omega}\right)-\beta_{1}.

(33)

∎

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\omega>0
and
\bm{\upsilon}\in\mathcal{V}
. The following statements hold:

•

\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\cdot)
is
\omega
-smooth, i.e., for every
\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}{\in\Lambda}
it holds that:

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime})-\nabla_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\right|\leq\omega\left\|\bm{\lambda}-\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}\right\|_{2}^{2}

•

\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\cdot)
satisfies the PL condition, i.e., for every
\bm{\lambda}{\in\Lambda}
it holds that:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\|_{2}^{2}\geq\omega\left(\max_{\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}{\in\Lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\right).

•

\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\cdot)
satisfies the
error bound
(EB) condition, i.e., for every
\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}{\in\Lambda}
it holds that:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\|\geq\frac{\omega}{2}\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon})-\bm{\lambda}\|_{2},

where
\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon})=\argmax_{\bm{\lambda}\in\Lambda}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})
.

•

\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\cdot)
satisfies the
quadratic growth
(QG) condition, i.e., for every
\bm{\lambda},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}{\in\Lambda}
it holds that:

\displaystyle H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\geq\frac{\omega}{4}\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon})-\bm{\lambda}\|_{2},

where
\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon})=\argmax_{\bm{\lambda}\in\Lambda}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})
.

Proof.

For the first property, it is enough to observe that
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}
is twice differentiable in
\bm{\lambda}
and that its Hessian is
\omega\mathbf{I}
. For the second property, we observe that
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}
is quadratic in
\bm{\lambda}
and, consequently it satisfies the PL condition with parameter
\omega
:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\|_{2}^{2}\geq\omega\left(\max_{\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{U}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\right)\geq\omega\left(\max_{\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}{\in\Lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\right).

For the third and fourth properties, we refer to Lemma A.1 of
Yang et al. 2020
.
∎

Lemma A.6
.

Let
\omega>0
. For every
\bm{\upsilon}\in\mathcal{V}
, it holds that:

\displaystyle H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-H_{\omega}^{*}\geq\frac{\omega}{4}\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon})-\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega}\|_{2}.

(34)

Proof.

Let us consider the following derivation:

\displaystyle H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-H_{\omega}^{*}

\displaystyle=H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})

(35)

\displaystyle\geq H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})

(36)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\omega}{4}\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon})-\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega}\|_{2}.

(37)

having exploited the fact that, from the saddle point property,
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}^{*}_{\omega},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})\leq\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda}^{*}_{\omega})
and, then, Lemma
A.5
.
∎

Lemma A.7
.

Let
\omega>0
. The following statements hold:

•

H_{\omega}
is
L_{H}
-smooth, i.e., for every
\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\upsilon}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}
, it holds that:

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}^{\prime})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})\|_{2}\leq L_{H}\|\bm{\upsilon}^{\prime}-\bm{\upsilon}\|_{2}.

where
L_{H}\coloneqq L_{2}+\frac{L_{1}^{2}}{\omega}
.

•

For every
\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\upsilon}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}
we have
\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})=\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\rvert_{\bm{\lambda}=\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon})}
, where
\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon})=\argmax_{\bm{\lambda}\in\Lambda}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})
.

Proof.

The first and second statements follow from Lemma A.5 of
Nouiehed et al. 2019
.
∎

See
3.2

Proof.

The proof is subdivided into several parts. We will omit the
\omega
subscript for notational easiness. Let us focus on a specific iteration
k\in\mathbb{N}
.

Part I: bounding the
a_{k}
term.
Let us start with the
a_{k}
term:

\displaystyle H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-H^{*}

\displaystyle\leq H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-H^{*}+\left<\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1}-\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\;\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right>+\frac{L_{H}}{2}\left\|\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1}-\bm{\upsilon}_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}

(38)

\displaystyle\leq H_{\bm{\upsilon}}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-H^{*}-\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left<\widehat{\nabla}_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k}),\;\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right>

(39)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{L_{H}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}\left\|\widehat{\nabla}_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2},

(40)

where the first line is due to the fact that the function
H_{\omega}
is
L_{H}
-smooth (Lemma
A.7
), the last inequality is due to the update rule of
\bm{\upsilon}
.
Now, we apply the expected value on both sides of the inequality and we use the fact that the gradient estimation is unbiased and has variance bounded by
V_{\bm{\upsilon}}
:

\displaystyle\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]-H^{*}

\displaystyle\leq H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-H^{*}-\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left<\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k}),\;\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right>

(41)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{L_{H}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}\E\left[\left\|\widehat{\nabla}_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right],

(42)

where
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
is the filtration associated with all events realized up to interaction
k-1
.
We recall that:

\displaystyle\E\left[\left\|\widehat{\nabla}_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]=\Var\left[\widehat{\nabla}_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]+\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2},

(43)

and that
\Var\left[\widehat{\nabla}_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right]\leq V_{\bm{\upsilon}}
by Assumption
3.4
. Thus, selecting
\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\leq 1/L_{H}
, we have that:

\displaystyle\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]-H^{*}

(44)

\displaystyle\leq H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-H^{*}-\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left<\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\;\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right>

(45)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{L_{H}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\upsilon}}

(46)

\displaystyle=H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-H^{*}-\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left<\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\;\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right>

(47)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\pm\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{L_{H}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\upsilon}}.

(48)

Consider that:

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})+\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}

(49)

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}

(50)

\displaystyle\quad+\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left<\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k}),\;\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right>.

(51)

Thus, the following holds:

\displaystyle\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]-H^{*}

(52)

\displaystyle\leq H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-H^{*}-\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left<\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k}),\;\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right>+\frac{L_{H}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\upsilon}}

(53)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}

(54)

\displaystyle\quad+\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left<\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k}),\;\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right>

(55)

\displaystyle=H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-H^{*}-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}

(56)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{L_{H}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\upsilon}}.

(57)

Thus, we have obtained:

A

\displaystyle\coloneqq\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]-H^{*}

(58)

\displaystyle\leq H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-H^{*}-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}

(59)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{L_{H}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\upsilon}},

(60)

holding via the selection of
\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\leq 1/L_{H}
.
Notice that, from
A
the following directly follows:

D

\displaystyle\coloneqq\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]-H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})

(61)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{L_{H}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\upsilon}}.

(62)

Part II: bounding the
b_{k}
term.
We are ready to analyze the
b_{k}
term.
Recall that for ridge regularization of the Lagrangian function presented in the main paper, we have that
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}
is
\omega
-smooth and fulfills the PL condition with constant
\omega
, as shown in Lemma
A.5
. Since
\mathcal{L}
is a quadratic function of
\bm{\lambda}
and
\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})\in\Lambda
, we have that considering the non-projected
\bm{\lambda}_{k+1}
can only increase the distance. Thus, we will ignore projection for the rest of the proof.
We have:

\displaystyle H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k+1})

(63)

\displaystyle\leq H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\left<\bm{\lambda}_{k+1}-\bm{\lambda}_{k},\;\nabla_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right>+\frac{\omega}{2}\left\|\bm{\lambda}_{k+1}-\bm{\lambda}_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}

(64)

\displaystyle=H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}\left<\widehat{\nabla}_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k}),\;\nabla_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right>

(65)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}\left\|\widehat{\nabla}_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2},

(66)

that is possible under Assumption
3.3
(i.e.,
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}
is
L_{2}
-smooth) and due to the update rules we are considering.
Now, by applying the expectation on both sides, we obtain the following:

\displaystyle\E

\displaystyle\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k+1})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

(67)

\displaystyle\leq\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]-\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}

(68)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}\E\left[\left\|\widehat{\nabla}_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

(69)

\displaystyle\leq\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]-\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}

(70)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}\left\|\widehat{\nabla}_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda})\right\|_{2}^{2}

(71)

\displaystyle\leq\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}},

(72)

where the last line follows by selecting
\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}\leq 1/\omega
. Since
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}
enjoys the PL condition w.r.t
\bm{\lambda}
with constant
\omega
, for every pair
(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})
we have:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\omega\left(\max_{\overline{\bm{\lambda}}\in\mathbb{R}^{U}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\overline{\bm{\lambda}})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\right)\geq\omega\left(\max_{\overline{\bm{\lambda}}\in\Lambda}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\overline{\bm{\lambda}})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda})\right).

(73)

By applying the PL condition:

\displaystyle\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k+1})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

(74)

\displaystyle\leq\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\lambda}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}

(75)

\displaystyle\leq\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

(76)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}

(77)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]+\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}},

(78)

where we enforce
1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\geq 0
, i.e.,
\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}\leq 2/\omega
. However, we do not have a proper recursive term, thus consider the following:

\displaystyle H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})}_{\text{Recursive Term}}+\underbrace{\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})}_{\text{{C}}}

(79)

\displaystyle\qquad+\underbrace{H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})}_{\text{{D}}}.

(80)

A bound on
D
has already been derived, so let us bound the term
C
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})

\displaystyle-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\leq-\left<\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1}-\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\;\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right>+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\left\|\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1}-\bm{\upsilon}_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}

(81)

\displaystyle\leq\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left<\widehat{\nabla}_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k}),\;\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right>+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}\left\|\widehat{\nabla}_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2},

(82)

again because of Assumption
3.3
and the update rule.
Now, as usual, we consider the expectation conditioned to the filtration
\mathcal{F}_{k-1}
and the properties of the variance, to obtain:

C

\displaystyle\coloneqq\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\E\left[\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

(83)

\displaystyle\leq\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\upsilon}},

(84)

having set
\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\leq 1/L_{2}
.
We are finally able to conclude the bound of the term
B
:

B

\displaystyle\coloneqq\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k+1})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

(85)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]+\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}

(86)

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\left(H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right)

(87)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\left(\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\E\left[\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]\right)

(88)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\left(\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]-H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right)+\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}.

(89)

Now we apply the bounds on
C
and
D
(the latter is from Eq.
62
), obtaining:

\displaystyle\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k+1})|\mathcal{F}_{k-1}\right]

(90)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\left(H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right)

(91)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\left(\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\upsilon}}\right)

(92)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\left(-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right.

(93)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+\frac{L_{H}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\upsilon}}\right)+\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}},

(94)

that is the second fundamental term.

Part III: bounding the potential function
P_{k}(\chi)
.
Before going on, we recall that so far we enforced:
\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\leq 1/L_{H}
(since
L_{H}\geq L_{2}
) and
\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}\leq 1/\omega
, for every
t\in\mathopen{[\![}K\mathclose{]\!]}
.
What we want to bound here is the potential function
P_{k+1}(\chi)=a_{k+1}+\chi b_{k+1}
. Using the final results of Part I and Part II:

\displaystyle a_{k+1}

\displaystyle+\chi b_{k+1}=\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-H^{*}\right]+\chi\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k+1},\bm{\lambda}_{k+1})\right]

(95)

\displaystyle\leq\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-H^{*}\right]-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

(96)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]+\frac{L_{H}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\upsilon}}

(97)

\displaystyle\quad+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right]

(98)

\displaystyle\quad+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\left(\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\upsilon}}\right)

(99)

\displaystyle\quad+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\Bigg(-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

(100)

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]+\frac{L_{H}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\upsilon}}\Bigg)

(101)

\displaystyle\quad+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}

(102)

\displaystyle=a_{k}+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)b_{k}

(103)

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right)\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

(104)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right)\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

(105)

\displaystyle\quad+\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

(106)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}}{2}\left(L_{H}+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)(L_{H}+L_{2})\right)V_{\bm{\upsilon}}+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}.

(107)

Now we can re-arrange the terms by noticing that:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})+\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}

(108)

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}

(109)

\displaystyle\quad+\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\left<\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k}),\;\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right>

(110)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}+2\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2},

(111)

where the last inequality holds by Young’s inequality. Then we can write what follows:

\displaystyle a_{k+1}+\chi b_{k+1}

(112)

\displaystyle\leq a_{k}+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)b_{k}

(113)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right.

(114)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right)\right)\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

(115)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right)\right)

(116)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

(117)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}}{2}\left(L_{H}+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)(L_{H}+L_{2})\right)V_{\bm{\upsilon}}+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}.

(118)

Let us now proceed to bound
\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}
.
By Lemma
A.7
, we have that
\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})=\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon}))
for every
\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon})\in\argmax_{\overline{\bm{\lambda}}\in\Lambda}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\overline{\bm{\lambda}})
, thus we can write:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k}))\right\|_{2}^{2}

(119)

\displaystyle\leq L_{3}^{2}\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-\bm{\lambda}_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2},

(120)

since Assumption
3.3
holds.

For a fixed value of
\bm{\upsilon}
, by Lemma
A.5
it follows that
\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon},\cdot)
satisfies the quadratic growth condition (since it satisfies the PL condition), for which the following holds:

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{\lambda}^{*}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-\bm{\lambda}_{k}\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{4}{\omega}\left(H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right),

(121)

and thus we have:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\leq\frac{4L_{3}^{2}}{\omega}\left(H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right).

(122)

By applying the total expectation, it trivially follows:

\displaystyle\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})-\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]\leq\frac{4L_{3}^{2}}{\omega}\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-\mathcal{L}_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k},\bm{\lambda}_{k})\right]=\frac{4L_{3}^{2}}{\omega}b_{k}.

(123)

Thus, we have:

\displaystyle a_{k+1}+\chi b_{k+1}

(124)

\displaystyle\leq a_{k}+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)b_{k}

(125)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right.

(126)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right)\right)\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

(127)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right)\right)\frac{4L_{3}^{2}}{\omega}b_{k}

(128)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}}{2}\left(L_{H}+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)(L_{H}+L_{2})\right)V_{\bm{\upsilon}}+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}.

(129)

Part IV: apply the
\psi
-gradient domination.
Now we need to bound the term
\left\|\nabla H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}
. We consider Assumption
3.2
and we get:
\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{\psi}\geq\alpha_{1}\left(H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-H^{*}\right)-\beta_{1}
. By defining
\widetilde{H}^{*}\coloneqq H^{*}+\beta_{1}/\alpha_{1}
, we also have:

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})\right\|_{2}^{\psi}\geq\alpha_{1}

\displaystyle\max\left\{0,\;H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-\widetilde{H}^{*}\right\}

(130)

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})\right\|_{2}^{2}\geq\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}

\displaystyle\max\left\{0,\;H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-\widetilde{H}^{*}\right\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}.

If we apply the total expectation on both sides of the inequality, we get:

\displaystyle\E\left[\left\|\nabla_{\bm{\upsilon}}H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\E\left[\max\left\{0,\;H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-\widetilde{H}^{*}\right\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\right]

(131)

\displaystyle\geq\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\E\left[\max\left\{0,\;H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-\widetilde{H}^{*}\right\}\right]^{\frac{2}{\psi}}

(132)

\displaystyle\geq\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\max\left\{0,\;\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-\widetilde{H}^{*}\right]\right\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}},

(133)

which is achieved by a double application of Jensen’s inequality, since
z^{2/\psi}
is convex for
\psi\in[1,2]
and
z\geq 0
, and the maximum is convex.
Let us start from Equation (
124
):

\displaystyle a_{k+1}+\chi b_{k+1}

(134)

\displaystyle\leq a_{k}+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)b_{k}

(135)

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\left(2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right)\right)}_{\eqqcolon-C}

(136)

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\E\left[\left\|\nabla H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})\right\|_{2}^{2}\right]

(137)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right)\right)\frac{4L_{3}^{2}}{\omega}b_{k}

(138)

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}}{2}\left(L_{H}+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)(L_{H}+L_{2})\right)V_{\bm{\upsilon}}+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}}_{\eqqcolon V}.

(139)

We first enforce the negativity of
-C
. To this end:

\displaystyle-C=\left(2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\underbrace{\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)}_{\leq 3/2}\chi{\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)}-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi{\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)}\right)\right)

(140)

\displaystyle\leq\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(3\chi{\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)}-\frac{1}{2}\left(1+\chi{\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)}\right)\right)

(141)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(5\chi\underbrace{\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)}_{\leq 1}-1\right)\leq\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(5\chi-1\right)\leq 0.

(142)

Thus, it is enough to enforce
5\chi-1\leq 0\implies\chi\leq 1/5
. We now plug in the gradient domination inequalities:

\displaystyle a_{k+1}+\chi b_{k+1}

(143)

\displaystyle\leq a_{k}+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)b_{k}-C\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\max\left\{0;\;\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon})-\widetilde{H}^{*}\right]\right\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+V

(144)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right)\right)\frac{4L_{3}^{2}}{\omega}b_{k}.

(145)

Now we introduce the symbol
\widetilde{a}_{k}\coloneqq\E\left[H_{\omega}(\bm{\upsilon}_{k})-\widetilde{H}^{*}\right]=a_{k}-\beta_{1}/\alpha_{1}
, to get:

\displaystyle\widetilde{a}_{k+1}+\chi b_{k+1}

(146)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{a}_{k}-C\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\max\left\{0,\widetilde{a}_{k}\right\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+V

(147)

\displaystyle+\left(\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)+\left(2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right.\right.

(148)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.\left.+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right)\right)\frac{4L_{3}^{2}}{\omega}\right)b_{k}.

(149)

For what follows, we call
B
the term that is multiplying
b_{k}
:

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle\coloneqq\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)+\left(2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right.

(150)

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\right)\right)\frac{4L_{3}^{2}}{\omega}

(151)

Let refer to
\widetilde{a}_{k}+\chi b_{k}
as
\widetilde{P}_{t}(\chi)
with
\chi\in(0,1)
. For the sake of clarity, we re-write our main inequality as:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{t}(\chi)=\widetilde{a}_{k+1}+\chi b_{k+1}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{a}_{k}+Bb_{k}-C\max\left\{0;\;\widetilde{a}_{k}\right\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+V.

(152)

Then, from Lemma
B.1
, having set
a\leftarrow\widetilde{a}_{k}
and
b\leftarrow\chi b_{k}
, we have:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{t+1}(\chi)=\widetilde{a}_{k+1}+\chi b_{k+1}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{a}_{k}+Bb_{k}+C(\chi b_{k})^{\frac{2}{\psi}}-2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}C\max\left\{0,\;\widetilde{a}_{k}+\chi b_{k}\right\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+V.

(153)

By choosing
\chi
so that
\chi b_{k}\leq 1
, i.e.,
\chi\leq 1/\max_{k\in[K]}b_{k}
, we have:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{t+1}(\chi)=\widetilde{a}_{k+1}+\chi b_{k+1}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{a}_{k}+Bb_{k}+C(\chi b_{k})^{\frac{2}{\psi}}-2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}C\max\left\{0,\;\widetilde{a}_{k}+\chi b_{k}\right\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+V

(154)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{a}_{k}+(B+\chi C)b_{k}-2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}C\max\left\{0,\;\widetilde{a}_{k}+\chi b_{k}\right\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+V

(155)

\displaystyle=\widetilde{P}_{t}(B+\chi C)-2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}C\max\left\{0,\;\widetilde{P}_{t}(\chi)\right\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+V.

(156)

To unfold the recursion, we need to ensure that
B+\chi C\leq\chi
, which leads to a condition relating the two learning rates:

\displaystyle B+\chi C

(157)

\displaystyle=\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)+\left(2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\underbrace{\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)}_{\leq 3/2}\chi\underbrace{\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)}_{\leq 1}\right.

(158)

\displaystyle\qquad\left.+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\underbrace{\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)}_{\leq 1}\right)\right)\frac{4L_{3}^{2}}{\omega}

(159)

\displaystyle\quad+\chi{{\left(\underbrace{-2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)}_{\leq 0}+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi\underbrace{\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)}_{\leq 1}\right)\right)}\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}}

(160)

\displaystyle\leq\chi-\chi\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega+\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\left(\frac{2L_{3}^{2}}{\omega}\left(1+7\chi\right)+\frac{1+\chi}{2}\alpha_{2}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\right)\leq\chi

(161)

\displaystyle\implies\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\leq\frac{\omega^{2}\chi\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{(1+\chi)\omega\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+4L^{2}_{3}(1+7\chi)},

(162)

where we exploited
\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\leq 1/L_{2}
and
\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}\leq 2/\omega
. Thus, we have:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{k+1}(\chi)\leq\widetilde{P}_{k}(\chi)-2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}C\max\left\{0,\;\widetilde{P}_{k}(\chi)\right\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+V.

(163)

Collecting all conditions on the learning rates, we have:

\displaystyle\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{L_{H}},\frac{1}{L_{2}},\frac{\omega^{2}\chi\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{(1+\chi)\omega\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+4L^{2}_{3}(1+7\chi)}\right\},

(164)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{\omega},\frac{2}{\omega}\right\}=\frac{1}{\omega}.

(165)

As a further simplification, let us observe that:

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=\left(-2\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}{\underbrace{\left(1+\frac{L_{2}}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\right)}_{\leq 3/2}}\chi{\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)}+\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+\chi{\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)}\right)\right)\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}

(166)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}}{2}\left(1+5\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)\chi\right)\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\geq\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}}{2}.

(167)

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}}{2}\left(L_{H}+\chi\left(1-\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{2}\omega\right)(L_{H}+L_{2})\right)V_{\bm{\upsilon}}+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}

(168)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}}{2}\left((1+2\chi)L_{2}+{(1+\chi)}\frac{L_{1}^{2}}{\omega}\right)V_{\bm{\upsilon}}+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}\eqqcolon\widetilde{V}.

(169)

Denoting with
\widetilde{C}\eqqcolon 2^{1-\frac{1}{\psi}}\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}}{2}
, we are going to study the recurrence:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{k+1}(\chi)\leq\widetilde{P}_{k}(\chi)-\widetilde{C}\max\left\{0,\;\widetilde{P}_{k}(\chi)\right\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+\widetilde{V}.

(170)

Part V: Rates Computation

Part V(a): Exact gradients
We consider the case
\widetilde{V}=0
. Let us start with
\psi=2
. From Lemma
C.3
, we have:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{K}(\xi)\leq\left(1-\widetilde{C}\right)^{K}\widetilde{P}_{0}(\xi)\leq\epsilon

(171)

\displaystyle\implies K\leq\frac{\log\frac{\widetilde{P}_{0}(\xi)}{\epsilon}}{\log\frac{1}{1-\widetilde{C}}}\leq\widetilde{C}^{-1}\log\frac{\widetilde{P}_{0}(\xi)}{\epsilon}=\frac{2\log\frac{\widetilde{P}_{0}(\xi)}{\epsilon}}{2^{1-\frac{1}{\psi}}\zeta_{\bm{\rho},t}\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}}

(172)

The inequality on
K
holds under the conditions:

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{\psi\widetilde{P}_{0}(\chi)^{\frac{2}{\psi}-1}}\implies\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\leq\frac{2^{1+\frac{2}{\psi}}}{\psi\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\widetilde{P}_{0}(\chi)^{\frac{2}{\psi}-1}},

(173)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{L_{H}},\frac{1}{L_{2}},\frac{\omega^{2}\chi\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{(1+\chi)\omega\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+4L^{2}_{3}(1+7\chi)}\right\}

(174)

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\frac{1}{L_{2}+\frac{L_{1}^{2}}{\omega}},\frac{\omega^{2}\chi\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}}{(1+\chi)\omega\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+4L^{2}_{3}(1+7\chi)}\right\},

(175)

\displaystyle\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\omega},

(176)

where the first one derives from the hypothesis of Lemma
C.3
and the other two from the conditions on the learning rates derived in the previous parts. We set:

\displaystyle\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}

\displaystyle=\omega^{-1},

\displaystyle\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}

\displaystyle=\min\left\{\frac{2^{1+\frac{2}{\psi}}}{\psi\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\widetilde{P}_{0}(\chi)^{\frac{2}{\psi}-1}},\frac{1}{L_{2}+\frac{L_{1}^{2}}{\omega}},\frac{\omega\chi}{(1+\chi)\omega\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+4L^{2}_{3}(1+7\chi)}\right\}=\mathcal{O}(\omega).

Thus, the sample complexity becomes
K=\mathcal{O}\left(\omega^{-1}\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}\right)
.

Consider now
\psi\in[1,2)
. We have from Lemma
C.3
:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{K}(\chi)\leq\left(\left(\frac{2}{\psi}-1\right)\widetilde{C}K\right)^{-\frac{\psi}{2-\psi}}\leq\epsilon

(177)

\displaystyle\implies K\leq\frac{\psi}{2-\psi}\widetilde{C}^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{2}{\psi}+1}=\frac{2\psi}{(2-\psi){2^{1-\frac{1}{\psi}}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}}}\epsilon^{-\frac{2}{\psi}+1},

(178)

holding under the same conditions as before. With the same choices of learning rates, we obtain the sample complexity
K=\mathcal{O}\left(\omega^{-1}\epsilon^{-\frac{2}{\psi}+1}\right)
as sample complexity.

Part V(b): Estimated gradients
We consider
\widetilde{V}>0
. In this case, from Lemma
C.5
, we have:

\displaystyle\widetilde{P}_{K}(\chi)\leq\left(1-\widetilde{C}^{1-\frac{\psi}{2}}\widetilde{V}^{\frac{\psi}{2}}\right)^{K}\widetilde{P}_{0}(\chi)+\left(\frac{\widetilde{V}}{\widetilde{C}}\right)^{\frac{\psi}{2}}.

(179)

We enforce both terms to be smaller or equal to
\epsilon/2
. With the first one, we can evaluate the sample complexity:

\displaystyle\left(1-\widetilde{V}^{1-\frac{\psi}{2}}\widetilde{C}^{\frac{\psi}{2}}\right)^{K}\widetilde{P}_{0}(\chi)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}

(180)

\displaystyle\implies K\leq\frac{\log\frac{2\widetilde{P}_{0}(\chi)}{\epsilon}}{\widetilde{V}^{1-\frac{\psi}{2}}\widetilde{C}^{\frac{\psi}{2}}}

(181)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad=\frac{\log\frac{2\widetilde{P}_{0}(\chi)}{\epsilon}}{\left(\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}}{2}\left((1+2\chi)L_{2}+{(1+\chi)}\frac{L_{1}^{2}}{\omega}\right)V_{\bm{\upsilon}}+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}\right)^{1-\frac{\psi}{2}}\left(2^{1-\frac{1}{\psi}}\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}}{2}\right)^{\frac{\psi}{2}}}

(182)

Regarding the second one, we have:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\widetilde{V}}{\widetilde{C}}\right)^{\frac{\psi}{2}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\implies\left(\frac{\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}^{2}}{2}\left((1+2\chi)L_{2}+{(1+\chi)}\frac{L_{1}^{2}}{\omega}\right)V_{\bm{\upsilon}}+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}}{2^{1-\frac{1}{\psi}}\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}\alpha_{1}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}}{2}}\right)^{\frac{\psi}{2}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}

(183)

By enforcing the relation between the two learning rates, we set
\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon},k}=\mathcal{O}(\omega^{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda},k})
. By enforcing the previous inequality, recalling that
L_{2}\leq\mathcal{O}(\omega^{-1})
and
V_{\bm{\upsilon}}\leq\mathcal{O}(\omega^{-2})
, we obtain
\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}=\mathcal{O}(\omega\epsilon^{2/\psi})
, from which
\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon}}=\mathcal{O}(\omega^{3}\epsilon^{2/\psi})
. Substituting these values into the sample complexity upper bound, we get (highlighting the terms possibly depending on
\omega
):

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}}{((L_{2}+\omega^{-1})V_{\bm{\upsilon}}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon}}^{2}+\omega\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}^{2})^{1-\psi/2}\zeta_{\bm{\upsilon}}^{\psi/2}}\right)

(184)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}}{((L_{2}+\omega^{-1})V_{\bm{\upsilon}}(\omega^{3}\epsilon^{2/\psi})^{2}+\omega(\omega\epsilon^{2/\psi})^{2})^{1-\psi/2}(\omega^{3}\epsilon^{2/\psi})^{\psi/2}}\right)

(185)

\displaystyle\leq\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}}{\omega^{3}\epsilon^{4/\psi-1}}\right),

(186)

having bounded the sum at the denominator with the second addendum.

∎

See
4.6

Proof.

Under the considered set of assumptions, we recover the results of Theorems
4.2
,
4.3
, and
4.5
and of Lemma
4.4
, matching the conditions needed to establish the sample complexity exhibited in Theorem
3.2
for ensuring that
\mathcal{P}_{\dagger,K}(\chi)\leq\epsilon+\frac{\beta_{\dagger}(\sigma.\psi)}{\alpha_{\text{D}}}
, where we employed the coefficients of the inherited weak
\psi
-GD (Theorem
4.2
).

In particular, recovering “Part V: Rates Computation” of the proof of Theorem
3.2
with
\psi\in[1,2]
and inexact gradients, to compute the sample complexity needed to ensure last-iterate global convergence of
\mathcal{P}_{\dagger,K}(\chi)
, we have to ensure the following conditions:

(
i
).

K\leq\frac{\log\frac{2\widetilde{P}_{\dagger,0}(\chi)}{\epsilon}}{\left(\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}^{2}}{2}\left((1+2\chi)L_{2}+{(1+\chi)}\frac{L_{1}^{2}}{\omega}\right)V_{\dagger,\bm{\theta}}+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}\right)^{1-\frac{\psi}{2}}\left(2^{1-\frac{1}{\psi}}\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}\alpha_{\text{D}}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}}{2}\right)^{\frac{\psi}{2}}}
,

(
ii
).

\left(\frac{\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}^{2}}{2}\left((1+2\chi)L_{2}+{(1+\chi)}\frac{L_{1}^{2}}{\omega}\right)V_{\dagger,\bm{\theta}}+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}}{2^{1-\frac{1}{\psi}}\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}\alpha_{\text{D}}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}}{2}}\right)^{\frac{\psi}{2}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}
,

where
L_{1}=\mathcal{O}(1)
and
L_{2}=\mathcal{O}(\omega^{-1})
are quantified in Theorem
4.3
, while
V_{\dagger,\bm{\theta}}=\mathcal{O}(\omega^{-2}\sigma^{-2})
and
V_{\bm{\lambda}}=\mathcal{O}(1)
are quantified in Lemma
4.4
.

Now, considering
(ii)
, by enforcing the relation between the two learning rates specified in Theorem
3.2
, we can set
\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}=\mathcal{O}(\omega^{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}})
. Exploiting the characterization of
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
(Theorem
4.3
) and the one of
V_{\dagger,\bm{\theta}}
and
V_{\bm{\lambda}}
(Lemma
4.4
), we have the following:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}^{2}}{2}\left((1+2\chi)L_{2}+{(1+\chi)}\frac{L_{1}^{2}}{\omega}\right)V_{\dagger,\bm{\theta}}+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}}{2^{1-\frac{1}{\psi}}\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}\alpha_{\text{D}}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}}{2}}\right)^{\frac{\psi}{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\omega^{4}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}^{2}\left(\omega^{-1}+\omega^{-1}\right)\omega^{-2}\sigma^{-2}+\omega\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}^{2}}{\omega^{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}}\right)^{\frac{\psi}{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\omega\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}^{2}\sigma^{-2}+\omega\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}^{2}}{\omega^{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}}\right)^{\frac{\psi}{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}\omega^{-1}\sigma^{-2}\right)^{\frac{\psi}{2}}.

Thus, enforcing
\mathcal{O}\left(\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}\omega^{-1}\sigma^{-2}\right)^{\frac{\psi}{2}}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2}
, we have that
\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}=\mathcal{O}(\omega\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2/\psi})
, thus implying
\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}=\mathcal{O}(\omega^{3}\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2/\psi})
.

If we now consider the term
(i)
, by exploiting the form of
L_{1},L_{2},V_{\dagger,\bm{\theta}}
, and
V_{\bm{\lambda}}
and the choice of
\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}
and
\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}
, we have the following:

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\log\frac{2\widetilde{P}_{\dagger,0}(\chi)}{\epsilon}}{\left(\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}^{2}}{2}\left((1+2\chi)L_{2}+{(1+\chi)}\frac{L_{1}^{2}}{\omega}\right)V_{\dagger,\bm{\theta}}+\chi\frac{\omega}{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}^{2}V_{\bm{\lambda}}\right)^{1-\frac{\psi}{2}}\left(2^{1-\frac{1}{\psi}}\frac{\zeta_{\bm{\theta}}\alpha_{\text{D}}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}}{2}\right)^{\frac{\psi}{2}}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}}{\left(\omega\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}^{2}\sigma^{-2}\right)^{1-\psi/2}\left(\omega^{2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}\right)^{\psi/2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}}{\omega^{1+\psi/2}\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}^{2-\psi/2}\sigma^{\psi-2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{O}\left(\frac{\log\frac{1}{\epsilon}}{\omega^{3}\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{-1+4/\psi}}\right),

where in the last line, we exploited the fact that
\zeta_{\bm{\lambda}}=\mathcal{O}(\omega\sigma^{2}\epsilon^{2/\psi})
.

This iteration complexity, which naturally translates into a sample complexity given that we can employ a constant batch size
N
, ensures that
\mathcal{P}_{\dagger,K}(\chi)\leq\epsilon+\frac{\beta_{\dagger}(\sigma,\psi)}{\alpha_{\text{D}}}
. By leveraging the result of Theorem
4.5
, we have that the same sample complexity ensures that:

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\text{D},K}(\chi)\leq\epsilon+\frac{\beta_{\dagger}(\sigma,\psi)}{\alpha_{\text{D}}}+4(1+\Lambda_{\max})L_{1\dagger}\sigma\sqrt{d_{\dagger}}.

∎

Appendix B
Technical Lemmas

Lemma B.1
.

Let
a\in\mathbb{R}
,
b\geq 0
, and
\psi\in[1,2]
. It holds that:

\displaystyle\max\{0,a\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\geq 2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}\max\{0,a+b\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}-b^{\frac{2}{\psi}}.

(187)

Proof.

Let us consider the following derivation:

\displaystyle\max\{0,a\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}a^{\frac{2}{\psi}}&\text{if }a>0\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(188)

\displaystyle\geq\begin{cases}2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}(a+b)^{\frac{2}{\psi}}-b^{\frac{2}{\psi}}&\text{if }a>0\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(189)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}(a+b)^{\frac{2}{\psi}}-b^{\frac{2}{\psi}}&\text{if }a>0\\
0&\text{if }-b<a\leq 0\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(190)

\displaystyle\geq\begin{cases}2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}(a+b)^{\frac{2}{\psi}}-b^{\frac{2}{\psi}}&\text{if }a>0\\
2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}(a+b)^{\frac{2}{\psi}}-b^{\frac{2}{\psi}}&\text{if }-b<a\leq 0\\
-b^{\frac{2}{\psi}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(191)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}(a+b)^{\frac{2}{\psi}}-b^{\frac{2}{\psi}}&\text{if }a+b>0\\
-b^{\frac{2}{\psi}}&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

(192)

\displaystyle=2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}\max\{0,a+b\}^{\frac{2}{\psi}}-b^{\frac{2}{\psi}},

(193)

where the first inequality follows from
(x+y)^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\leq 2^{\frac{2}{\psi}-1}(x^{\frac{2}{\psi}}+y^{\frac{2}{\psi}})
for
x,y\geq 0
, from Holder’s inequality; the second inequality from observing that
2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}(a+b)^{\frac{2}{\psi}}-b^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\leq(2^{1-\frac{2}{\psi}}-1)b^{\frac{2}{\psi}}\leq 0
for
-b<a\leq 0
.
∎

Appendix C
Recurrences

In this section, we provide auxiliary results about convergence rate of a certain class of recurrences that will be employed for the convergence analysis of the proposed algorithms. Specifically, we study the recurrence:

\displaystyle r_{k+1}\leq r_{k}-a\max\{0,r_{k}\}^{\phi}+b

(195)

for
a>0
,
b\geq 0
, and
\phi\in[1,2]
. To this end, we consider the helper sequence:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\rho_{0}=r_{0}\\
\rho_{k+1}=\rho_{k}-a\max\{0,\rho_{k}\}^{\phi}+b\end{cases}

(196)

The line of the proof follows that of
Montenegro et al. 2024a
. Let us start showing that for sufficiently small
a
, the sequence
\rho_{k}
upper bounds
r_{k}
.

Lemma C.1
.

If
a\leq\frac{1}{\phi\rho_{k}^{\phi-1}}
for every
k\geq 0
, then,
r_{k}\leq\rho_{k}
for every
k\geq 0
.

Proof.

By induction on
k
. For
k=0
, the statement holds since
\rho_{0}=r_{0}
. Suppose the statement holds for every
j\leq k
, we prove that it holds for
k+1
:

\displaystyle\rho_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\rho_{k}-a\max\{0,\rho_{k}\}^{\phi}+b

(197)

\displaystyle\geq r_{k}-a\max\{0,r_{k}\}^{\phi}+b

(198)

\displaystyle\geq r_{k+1},

(199)

where the first inequality holds by the inductive hypothesis and by observing that the function
f(x)=x-a\max\{0,x\}^{\phi}
is non-decreasing in
x
when
a\leq\frac{1}{\phi\rho_{k}^{\phi-1}}
. Indeed, if
x<0
, then
f(x)=x
, which is non-decreasing; if
x\geq 0
, we have
f(x)=x-ax^{\phi}
, that can be proved to be non-decreasing in the interval
\left[0,(a\phi)^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}\right]
simply by studying the sign of the derivative. Thus, we enforce the following requirement to ensure that
\rho_{k}
falls in the non-decreasing region:

\displaystyle\rho_{k}\leq(a\phi)^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}\implies a\leq\frac{1}{\phi\rho_{k}^{\phi-1}}.

(200)

So does
r_{k}
by the inductive hypothesis.
∎

Thus, from now on, we study the properties of the sequence
\rho_{k}
. Let us note that, if
\rho_{k}
is convergent, then it converges to the fixed-point
\overline{\rho}
computed as follows:

\displaystyle\overline{\rho}=\overline{\rho}-a\max\{0,\overline{\rho}\}^{\phi}+b\implies\overline{\rho}=\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{\phi}},

(201)

having retained the positive solution of the equation only, since the negative one never attains the maximum
\max\{0,\overline{\rho}\}
.
Let us now study the monotonicity properties of the sequence
\rho_{k}
.

Lemma C.2
.

The following statements hold:

•

If
r_{0}>\overline{\rho}
and
a\leq\frac{1}{\phi r_{0}^{\phi-1}}
, then for every
k\geq 0
it holds that:
\overline{\rho}\leq\rho_{k+1}\leq\rho_{k}
.

•

If
r_{0}<\overline{\rho}
and
a\leq\frac{1}{\phi\overline{\rho}^{\phi-1}}
, then for every
k\geq 0
it holds that:
\overline{\rho}\geq\rho_{k+1}\geq\rho_{k}
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to that of
(
Montenegro et al. 2024a
, Lemma F.3)
.
∎

From now on, we focus on the case in which
r_{0}\geq\overline{\rho}
, since, as we shall see later, the opposite case is irrelevant for the convergence guarantees.
We now consider two cases:
b=0
and
b>0
.

C.1
Analysis when
b=0

From the policy optimization perspective, this case corresponds to the one in which the gradients are exact (no variance). Recall that here
\overline{\rho}=0
. We have the following convergence result.

Lemma C.3
.

If
a\leq\frac{1}{\phi r_{0}^{\phi-1}}
,
r_{0}\geq 0
, and
b=0
it holds that:

\displaystyle\rho_{k+1}\leq\begin{cases}(1-a)^{k+1}r_{0}&\text{if }\phi=1\\
\min\left\{r_{0},((\phi-1)a(k+1))^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}\right\}&\text{if }\phi\in(1,2]\end{cases}.

(202)

Proof.

Since
r_{0}\geq 0=\overline{\rho}
, from Lemma
C.2
, we know that
\rho_{k}\geq 0
and, thus,
\max\{0,\rho_{k}\}=\rho_{k}
. For
\phi=1
, we have:

\displaystyle\rho_{k+1}=\rho_{k}-a\rho_{k}=(1-a)\rho_{k}=(1-a)^{k+1}\rho_{0}=(1-a)^{k+1}r_{0}.

(203)

For
\phi\in(1,2]
, we have:

\displaystyle\rho_{k+1}=\rho_{k}-a\rho_{k}^{\phi}.

(204)

We proceed by induction. For
k=0
, the statement hold since
\rho_{0}=r_{0}
and
r_{0}\leq(\phi a)^{-\frac{1}{\psi-1}}\leq((\phi-1)a)^{-\frac{1}{\psi-1}}
from the condition on the learning rate. Suppose the thesis holds for
j\leq k
, we prove it for
k+1
.
\rho_{k+1}\leq r_{0}
by monotonicity, and, from the inductive hypothesis:

\displaystyle\rho_{k+1}=\rho_{k}-a\rho_{k}^{\phi}

\displaystyle\leq(\phi ak)^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}-a(\phi ak)^{-\frac{\phi}{\phi-1}}

(205)

\displaystyle=\underbrace{(\phi ak)^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}-(\phi a(k+1))^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}-a(\phi ak)^{-\frac{\phi}{\phi-1}}}_{(*)}+(\phi a(k+1))^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}.

(206)

We now prove that
(*)
is non-positive:

\displaystyle(*)

\displaystyle=((\phi-1)ak)^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}-((\phi-1)a(k+1))^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}-a((\phi-1)ak)^{-\frac{\phi}{\phi-1}}

(207)

\displaystyle=((\phi-1)a)^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}k^{-\frac{\phi}{\phi-1}}\underbrace{\left(k-(k+1)\left(\frac{k}{k+1}\right)^{\frac{\phi}{\phi-1}}\right)}_{\leq\frac{1}{\phi-1}}-a^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}((\phi-1)k)^{-\frac{\phi}{\phi-1}}

(208)

\displaystyle\leq a^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}k^{-\frac{\phi}{\phi-1}}(\phi-1)^{-\frac{1}{\phi-1}}\left(\frac{1}{\phi-1}-\frac{1}{\phi-1}\right)\leq 0,

(209)

having observed that:

\displaystyle\sup_{k\geq 1}\left(k-(k+1)\left(\frac{k}{k+1}\right)^{\frac{\phi}{\phi-1}}\right)=\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\left(k-(k+1)\left(\frac{k}{k+1}\right)^{\frac{\phi}{\phi-1}}\right)=\frac{1}{\phi-1}.

(210)

∎

C.2
Analysis for
b>0

From the policy optimization perspective, this corresponds to the case in which the gradients are estimated, i.e., the variance is positive. In this case, we proceed considering the helper sequence:

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\eta_{0}=\rho_{0}\\
\eta_{k+1}=\left(1-a\overline{\rho}^{\phi-1}\right)\eta_{k}+b&\text{if }k\geq 0\end{cases}.

(211)

We show that the sequence
\eta_{k}
upper bounds
\rho_{k}
when
\rho_{0}=r_{0}\geq\overline{\rho}
.

Lemma C.4
.

If
r_{0}>\overline{\rho}
and
a\leq\frac{1}{\phi r_{0}^{\phi-1}}
, then, for every
k\geq 0
, it holds that
\eta_{k}\geq\rho_{k}
.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to that of
(
Montenegro et al. 2024a
, Lemma F.4)
.
∎

Thus, we can provide the convergence guarantee.

Lemma C.5
.

If
a\leq\frac{1}{\phi r_{0}^{\phi-1}}
,
r_{0}\geq 0
, and
b>0
it holds that:

\displaystyle\eta_{k+1}\leq\left(1-b^{1-\frac{1}{\phi}}a^{\frac{1}{\phi}}\right)^{k+1}+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{\phi}}.

(212)

Proof.

By unrolling the recursion:

\displaystyle\eta_{k+1}

\displaystyle=\left(1-a\overline{\rho}^{\phi-1}\right)\eta_{k}+b

(213)

\displaystyle=\left(1-a\overline{\rho}^{\phi-1}\right)^{k+1}r_{0}+b\sum_{j=0}^{k}\left(1-a\overline{\rho}^{\phi-1}\right)^{j}

(214)

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-a\overline{\rho}^{\phi-1}\right)^{k+1}r_{0}+b\sum_{j=0}^{+\infty}\left(1-a\overline{\rho}^{\phi-1}\right)^{j}

(215)

\displaystyle=\left(1-b^{1-\frac{1}{\phi}}a^{\frac{1}{\phi}}\right)^{k+1}+\frac{b}{a\overline{\rho}^{\phi-1}}

(216)

\displaystyle=\left(1-b^{1-\frac{1}{\phi}}a^{\frac{1}{\phi}}\right)^{k+1}+\left(\frac{b}{a}\right)^{\frac{1}{\phi}}.

(217)

∎

Appendix D
Experimental Details

D.1
Employed Policies and Hyperpolicies

Linear Gaussian Policy.

A
linear parametric gaussian
policy
\pi_{\bm{\theta}}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\to\Delta(\mathcal{A})
with variance
\sigma^{2}
samples the actions as
a_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{\theta}^{\top}\bm{s}_{t},\sigma^{2}I_{d_{\mathcal{S}}})
, where
\bm{s}_{t}
is the observed state at time
t
and
\bm{\theta}
is the parameter vector.

Tabular Softmax Policy.

A
tabular softmax
policy
\pi_{\bm{\theta}}:\mathcal{S}\times\mathcal{A}\to\Delta(\mathcal{A})
with a constant temperature
\tau
is such that:

\displaystyle\pi_{\bm{\theta}}(\bm{a}_{j}|\bm{s}_{i})=\frac{\exp\left(\frac{\bm{\theta}_{i,j}}{\tau}\right)}{\sum_{z=1}^{|\mathcal{A}|}\exp\left(\frac{\bm{\theta}_{i,z}}{\tau}\right)},

where
\bm{\theta}_{i,j}
is the parameter associated with the
i
-th state and the
j
-th action. Notice that the total number of parameters for this kind of policy is
|\mathcal{S}||\mathcal{A}|
.

Linear Deterministic Policy.

A
linear parametric deterministic
policy
\mu_{\bm{\theta}}:\mathcal{S}\to\mathcal{A}
samples the actions as
\bm{a}_{t}=\bm{\theta}^{\top}\bm{s}_{t}
, where
\bm{s}_{t}
is the observed state at time
t
and
\bm{\theta}
is the parameter vector.

Gaussian Hyperpolicy.

A
parametric gaussian
hyperpolicy
\nu_{\bm{\rho}}\in\Delta(\Theta)
with variance
\sigma^{2}
samples the parameters
\bm{\theta}
for the underlying generic parametric policy
\pi_{\bm{\theta}}
as
\bm{\theta}_{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(\bm{\rho},\sigma^{2}I_{d_{\Theta}})
, where
\bm{\rho}
is the parameter vector for the hyperpolicy.

D.2
Environments

Discrete Grid World with Walls.

Discrete Grid World with Walls (DGWW) is a simple discrete environment we employed to compare
C-PGAE
against the sample-based versions of NPG-PD
(
Ding et al. 2020
, Appendix H)
and RPG-PD
(
Ding et al. 2024
, Appendix C.9)
.
DGWW is a grid-like bidimensional environment in which an agent can assume only integer coordinate positions and in which an agent can play four actions stating whether to go up, right, left, or down.
The goal is to reach the center of the grid performing the minimum amount of steps, begin the initial state uniformly sampled among the four vertices of the grid.
The agent is rewarded negatively and proportionally to its distance from the center, where the reward is
0
.
Around the goal state there is a “U-shaped” obstacle with an opening on the top side.
In particular, when the agent lands in a state in which the wall is present, it receives a cost of
1
, otherwise the cost signal is always equal to
0
.
In our experiments, we employed a DGWW environment of such a kind, with
|\mathcal{S}|=49
, i.e., with each dimension with length equal to
7
.

Linear Quadratic Regulator with Costs.

The Linear Quadratic Regulator
(
Anderson and Moore 2007
, LQR,)
is a continuous environment we employed in the regularization sensitivity study of
C-PGAE
and
C-PGPE
, and in the comparison among the same algorithms against the sample-based version of NPG-PD2
(
Ding et al. 2022
, Algorithm 1)
and its ridge-regularized version RPG-PD2 (not provided by the authors, but designed by us).
LQR is a dynamical system governed by the following state evolution:

\displaystyle\bm{s}_{t+1}=A\bm{s}_{t}+B\bm{a}_{t},

where
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{S}}\times d_{\mathcal{S}}}
and
B\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{S}}\times d_{\mathcal{A}}}
.

In the standard version of the environment, the reward is computed at each step as:

\displaystyle r_{t}=-\bm{s}_{t}^{\top}R\bm{s}_{t}-\bm{a}_{t}^{\top}Q\bm{a}_{t},

where
R\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{S}}\times d_{\mathcal{S}}}
and
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{A}}\times d_{\mathcal{A}}}
.

We modified this version of the LQR environment introducing costs.
In particular, in our
CostLQR
, the state evolution is treated as in the original case, while the reward at step
t
is computed as:

\displaystyle r_{t}=-\bm{s}_{t}^{\top}R\bm{s}_{t},

where
R\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{S}}\times d_{\mathcal{S}}}
.
Moreover, we added a cost signal
c
which is computed as follows at every time step
t
:

\displaystyle c_{t}=\bm{a}_{t}^{\top}Q\bm{a}_{t},

where
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{\mathcal{A}}\times d_{\mathcal{A}}}
.

In our experiments, we consider a
CostLQR
environment whose main characteristics are reported in Table
4
.

Additionally, we considered a uniform initial state distribution in
[-3,3]
and the following matrices:

\displaystyle A=B=0.9\left[\begin{matrix}1&0\\
0&1\end{matrix}\right],\qquad Q=\left[\begin{matrix}0.9&0\\
0&0.1\end{matrix}\right],\qquad R=\left[\begin{matrix}0.1&0\\
0&0.9\end{matrix}\right].

MuJoCo with Costs.

For our experiments on risk minimization, we utilized environments from the MuJoCo control suite
(
Todorov et al. 2012
)
, which offers a variety of continuous control environments. To tailor these environments to our specific requirements, we introduced a cost function that represents the energy associated with the control actions. In standard MuJoCo environments, a portion of the reward is typically calculated as the cost of the control action, which is proportional to the deviation of the chosen action from predefined action bounds.
In our MuJoCo modification, at each time step, we make the environment return a cost computed as:

\displaystyle\left\|\bm{a}_{t}-\min\left\{\max\left\{\bm{a}_{t},a_{\min}\right\},a_{\max}\right\}\right\|_{2},

where
a_{\min}
and
a_{\max}
are, respectively, the bounds for the minimum and maximum value for each component of the action vector.
Then, the action
\min\left\{\max\left\{\bm{a}_{t},a_{\min}\right\},a_{\max}\right\}
is passed to the environment.
In our experiment, we consider
Swimmer-v4
and
Hopper-v4
MuJoCo environments, whose main features are summarized in Table
4
.

RobotWorld.

For the comparison among
C-PG
, AD-PGPD
(
Rozada et al. 2025
)
, and PGDual
(
Zhao and You 2021
;
Brunke et al. 2022
)
, we employed the
Robot World
environment
(
Rozada et al. 2025
)
. This environment is a modification of the
CostLQR
one in which both reward and cost functions are given by the following quadratic functions:

\displaystyle r(\bm{s},\bm{a})

\displaystyle=\left<G_{1};\left|\bm{s}\right|\right>+\left<R_{1};\left|\bm{a}\right|\right>-\frac{1}{2}\|\bm{a}\|^{2}_{2},

\displaystyle c(\bm{s},\bm{a})

\displaystyle=\left<G_{2};\bm{s}^{2}\right>+\left<R_{2};\bm{a}^{2}\right>,

where for every
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
we define
|\bm{x}|\coloneqq(|x_{1}|,\ldots,|x_{n}|)^{\top}
and
\bm{x}^{2}\coloneqq(x_{1}^{2},\ldots,x_{n}^{2})^{\top}
. In the setting we considered for our experiments, we employed the following values for
G_{1},G_{2},R_{1}
, and
R_{2}
:

\displaystyle G_{1}=-(1,1,0.001,0.001),\quad G_{2}=-(0.001,0.001,1,1),\quad\text{and}\quad R_{1}=R_{2}=-(0.01,0.01).

Furthermore, differently from the usual LQR environment, in
RobotWorld
the agent is allowed to control both velocity and acceleration of the agent.

,

.
